An interface\\n    81\\t    whose order can be wrong should not be expressed as an order.\\n    82\\t    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    83\\t\\n    84\\t    em: float\\n    85\\t    f1: float\\n    86\\t    prec: float\\n    87\\t    recall: float\\n    88\\t\\n    89\\t\\n    90\\tdef hotpot_update_sp(sp_prediction: Sequence[Sequence[Any]] | None,\\n    91\\t                     sp_gold: Sequence[Sequence[Any]] | None) -> SpScore:\\n    92\\t    \\\"\\\"\\\"`hotpot_evaluate_v1.py::update_sp`, with the missing-field guard.\\n    93\\t\\n    94\\t    `em = 1.0 if fp + fn == 0 else 0.0` -- exact set equality, not F1.\\n    95\\t    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    96\\t    if not sp_prediction:\\n    97\\t        raise MissingEvidence(\\n    98\\t            \\\"HotpotQA supporting-fact evidence is absent; the contract treats this \\\"\\n    99\\t            \\\"as an incorrect prediction when scoring, rather than an invalid artifact.\\\")\\n   100\\t    cur = set(map(tuple, sp_prediction))\\n   101\\t    gold = set(map(tuple, sp_gold or []))\\n   102\\t    tp = sum(1 for e in cur if e in gold)\\n   103\\t    fp = sum(1 for e in cur if e not in gold)\\n   104\\t    fn = sum(1 for e in gold if e not in cur)\\n   105\\t    prec = tp / (tp + fp) if tp + fp > 0 else 0.0\\n   106\\t    recall = tp / (tp + fn) if tp + fn > 0 else 0.0\\n   107\\t    f1 = 2 * prec * recall / (prec + recall) if prec + recall > 0 else 0.0\\n   108\\t    return SpScore(em=1.0 if fp + fn == 0 else 0.0, f1=f1, prec=prec, recall=recall)\\n   109\\t\\n   110\\t\\n   111\\tdef hotpot_joint(em: float, prec: float, recall: float, sp: SpScore) -> tuple[float, float]:\\n   112\\t    \\\"\\\"\\\"`hotpot_evaluate_v1.py::eval`'s joint lines, verbatim.\\n   113\\t\\n   114\\t    `joint_em = em * sp_em`; `joint_prec = prec * sp_prec`, `joint_recall = recall *\\n   115\\t    sp_recall`, and `joint_f1` is the harmonic mean of the two joint components.\\n   116\\t    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n   117\\t    joint_prec = prec * sp.prec\\n   118\\t    joint_recall = recall * sp.recall\\n   119\\t    joint_f1 = (2 * joint_prec * joint_recall / (joint_prec + joint_recall)\\n   120\\t                if joint_prec + joint_recall > 0 else 0.0)\\n   121\\t    return em * sp.em, joint_f1\\n   122\\t\\n   123\\t\\n   124\\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------- FEVER ---\\n   125\\t# `sheffieldnlp/fever-scorer/src/fever/scorer.py`, transcribed.\\n   126\\t\\n   127\\t\\n   128\\tdef fever_is_correct_label(label: str, predicted_label: str) -> bool:\\n   129\\t    \\\"\\\"\\\"`scorer.py::is_correct_label`, verbatim.\\n   130\\t\\n   131\\t    `.upper()` and nothing else -- **no punctuation stripping**. Measured consequence,\\n   132\\t    pinned by the tests: `NOT ENOUGH INFO.` (one trailing full stop) is wrong. That is\\n   133\\t    upstream's strictness, not an implementation shortcut, and \\\"fixing\\\" it here would\\n   134\\t    produce a number higher than the published one.\\n   135\\t    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n   136\\t    return str(label).upper() == str(predicted_label).upper()\\n   137\\t\\n   138\\t\\n   139\\tdef fever_is_strictly_correct(label: str, predicted_label: str,\\n   140\\t                              evidence: Sequence[Any] | None,\\n   141\\t                              predicted_evidence: Sequence[Any] | None,\\n   142\\t                              *, max_evidence: int = 5) -> bool:\\n   143\\t    \\\"\\\"\\\"`scorer.py::is_strictly_correct`, with the missing-field guard.\\n   144\\t\\n   145\\t    A label is strictly correct when it is right **and** one whole gold evidence group is\\n   146\\t    contained in the first `max_evidence` predictions. `NOT ENOUGH INFO` items are exempt\\n   147\\t    from evidence -- that branch returns immediately, which is why an \\\"answer-only FEVER\\n   148\\t    score\\\" is not even the same rule for the three classes.\\n   149\\t    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n   150\\t    if str(label).upper() != \\\"NOT ENOUGH INFO\\\" and not predicted_evidence:\\n   151\\t        raise MissingEvidence(\\n   152\\t            \\\"FEVER predicted_evidence is absent; \\\"10.18653/v1/2020.emnlp-main.256\\\",\\n    35\\t    pages = \\\"3168--3177\\\",\\n    36\\t    abstract = \\\"Adversarial attacks reveal important vulnerabilities and flaws of trained models. One potent type of attack are universal adversarial triggers, which are individual n-grams that, when appended to instances of a class under attack, can trick a model into predicting a target class. However, for inference tasks such as fact checking, these triggers often inadvertently invert the meaning of instances they are inserted in. In addition, such attacks produce semantically nonsensical inputs, as they simply concatenate triggers to existing samples. Here, we investigate how to generate adversarial attacks against fact checking systems that preserve the ground truth meaning and are semantically valid. We extend the HotFlip attack algorithm used for universal trigger generation by jointly minimizing the target class loss of a fact checking model and the entailment class loss of an auxiliary natural language inference model. We then train a conditional language model to generate semantically valid statements, which include the found universal triggers. We find that the generated attacks maintain the directionality and semantic validity of the claim better than previous work.\\\"\\n    37\\t\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    38\\t\\n    39\\t# You can copy an official description\\n    40\\t_DESCRIPTION = \\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\\\n    41\\tDataset for training classification-only fact checking with claims from FEVER dataset.\\n    42\\tThis dataset is used in the paper \\\"Generating Label Cohesive and Well-Formed Adversarial Claims\\\", EMNLP 2020\\n    43\\t\\n    44\\tThe evidence is the gold evidence from the FEVER dataset for REFUTE and SUPPORT claims.\\n    45\\tFor NEI claims, we extract evidence sentences with the system in \\\"Christopher Malon. 2018.
